Well, I came in because I've started doing things at work and school lately that just do not make sense. I just catch myself in the middle of doing these things that don't make any sense.\n\nDoctor: Do these actions cause you any distress or interfere with your daily life?\n\nPatient: Yeah, they definitely do. It's getting in the way a lot. Like, for school, I actually failed an important course module recently. And at work... my ledgers have started coming back with serious errors. I've had to start asking for help because I just can't reliably finish work that used to be completely routine for me. So, yeah, it's definitely causing some problems.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any recurring thoughts that you can't seem to get out of your head?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. Um, I know it sounds really strange, but... I get convinced that certain invoice totals, account numbers, and due dates at work are actually messages about me. It just gets stuck in my head. And even when my supervisor sits down with me and shows me that they're just normal, everyday entries, I... I still feel like they're tracking whether I understand the pattern. I just can't seem to shake that thought.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever hear voices or sounds that other people can't hear?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I actually do. Um, sometimes when I'm just sitting alone at my desk, or when I'm walking home from class, I'll hear a man's voice. He'll say things like, \"check line seven\" or \"you missed the sign.\" It sounds real... like, it's clear enough that I actually look around to see who's talking to me, but there's never anyone there.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find it difficult to express your thoughts in a way that makes sense to others?\n\nPatient: Yeah, sometimes. Like... when I try to explain to my supervisor why a ledger is off, my mind just kind of goes everywhere. I'll start talking about the ledger, but then my thoughts jump to my tuition deadlines for school, and then to tax rules, and then somehow to studio lighting and the number patterns I've been seeing. I know what I'm trying to say, but people tell me they just can't follow how I got from one point to the next. It makes sense in my head, but it doesn't come out right.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your thoughts are often disjointed or hard to follow, even when you're not talking about the messages or voices you've mentioned?\n\nPatient: Yeah, exactly like I was just saying a second ago. It happens with normal stuff, too. If I'm just trying to explain why a ledger is off at work, my thoughts will jump from that to my tuition deadlines, and then to tax rules, and then to something completely random like studio lighting. Even if I'm not bringing up the messages or the number patterns, people still tell me they just cannot follow how I got from one point to the next.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself having trouble completing tasks or following a routine due to feeling disorganized?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. Actually, that's a big part of why I came in to get help. I've started doing things at work and school that just... don't make sense, and it stops me from getting my actual tasks done. \n\nLike, instead of doing my bookkeeping, I'll end up lining receipts all across the floor. Or I'll just sit at my desk tapping the calculator in sets of nine. Sometimes I'll even just stand by the copier with my hands held up in the air, and I'll stay like that until someone walks by and asks if I'm okay. So, yeah, it definitely makes it hard to follow a normal routine.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your thoughts are more disorganized than your speech, or is it the other way around?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure, honestly. I think... they kind of just happen together? Like, my thoughts feel scrambled, and then when I try to talk, the words come out scrambled, too. I haven't really noticed if one is worse than the other. They both just feel pretty disorganized right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your thoughts are often difficult to follow, even when you're thinking to yourself and not trying to explain them to someone else?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. He'll say things like, \"check line seven\" or \"you missed the sign.\" It sounds real... like, it's clear enough that I actually look around to see who's talking to me, but there's never anyone there.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find it difficult to express your thoughts in a way that makes sense to others?\n\nPatient: Yeah, sometimes. Like... when I try to explain to my supervisor why a ledger is off, my mind just kind of goes everywhere. I'll start talking about the ledger, but then my thoughts jump to my tuition deadlines for school, and then to tax rules, and then somehow to studio lighting and the number patterns I've been seeing. I know what I'm trying to say, but people tell me they just can't follow how I got from one point to the next. It makes sense in my head, but it doesn't come out right.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your thoughts are often disjointed or hard to follow, even when you're not talking about the messages or voices you've mentioned?\n\nPatient: Yeah, exactly like I was just saying a second ago. It happens with normal stuff, too. If I'm just trying to explain why a ledger is off at work, my thoughts will jump from that to my tuition deadlines, and then to tax rules, and then to something completely random like studio lighting. Even if I'm not bringing up the messages or the number patterns, people still tell me they just cannot follow how I got from one point to the next.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself having trouble completing tasks or following a routine due to feeling disorganized?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. Actually, that's a big part of why I came in to get help. I've started doing things at work and school that just... don't make sense, and it stops me from getting my actual tasks done. \n\nLike, instead of doing my bookkeeping, I'll end up lining receipts all across the floor. Or I'll just sit at my desk tapping the calculator in sets of nine. Sometimes I'll even just stand by the copier with my hands held up in the air, and I'll stay like that until someone walks by and asks if I'm okay. So, yeah, it definitely makes it hard to follow a normal routine.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your thoughts are more disorganized than your speech, or is it the other way around?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure, honestly. I think... they kind of just happen together? Like, my thoughts feel scrambled, and then when I try to talk, the words come out scrambled, too. I haven't really noticed if one is worse than the other. They both just feel pretty disorganized right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your thoughts are often difficult to follow, even when you're thinking to yourself and not trying to explain them to someone else?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. It's like I mentioned before... even when I'm just thinking about work, like trying to figure out why a ledger is off, my brain just starts jumping around. I'll go from the ledger to my tuition deadlines, then to tax rules, then to studio lighting or those number patterns. It all happens in my head, too, not just when I'm talking. But when I do try to explain it, people tell me they just can't follow how I got from one point to the next.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voices you hear or the messages you believe you're receiving could actually be real?\n\nPatient: I mean... yeah, I do. I know what I'm trying to say, but people tell me they just can't follow how I got from one point to the next. It makes sense in my head, but it doesn't come out right.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your thoughts are often disjointed or hard to follow, even when you're not talking about the messages or voices you've mentioned?\n\nPatient: Yeah, exactly like I was just saying a second ago. It happens with normal stuff, too. If I'm just trying to explain why a ledger is off at work, my thoughts will jump from that to my tuition deadlines, and then to tax rules, and then to something completely random like studio lighting. Even if I'm not bringing up the messages or the number patterns, people still tell me they just cannot follow how I got from one point to the next.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself having trouble completing tasks or following a routine due to feeling disorganized?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. Actually, that's a big part of why I came in to get help. I've started doing things at work and school that just... don't make sense, and it stops me from getting my actual tasks done. \n\nLike, instead of doing my bookkeeping, I'll end up lining receipts all across the floor. Or I'll just sit at my desk tapping the calculator in sets of nine. Sometimes I'll even just stand by the copier with my hands held up in the air, and I'll stay like that until someone walks by and asks if I'm okay. So, yeah, it definitely makes it hard to follow a normal routine.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your thoughts are more disorganized than your speech, or is it the other way around?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure, honestly. I think... they kind of just happen together? Like, my thoughts feel scrambled, and then when I try to talk, the words come out scrambled, too. I haven't really noticed if one is worse than the other. They both just feel pretty disorganized right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your thoughts are often difficult to follow, even when you're thinking to yourself and not trying to explain them to someone else?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. It's like I mentioned before... even when I'm just thinking about work, like trying to figure out why a ledger is off, my brain just starts jumping around. I'll go from the ledger to my tuition deadlines, then to tax rules, then to studio lighting or those number patterns. It all happens in my head, too, not just when I'm talking. But when I do try to explain it, people tell me they just can't follow how I got from one point to the next.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voices you hear or the messages you believe you're receiving could actually be real?\n\nPatient: I mean... yeah, I do. I know it sounds strange when I say it out loud, but I get completely convinced that certain invoice totals, account numbers, and due dates at work are actual messages about me. \n\nMy supervisor has actually sat down with me and showed me the paperwork to prove they're just normal, everyday entries. And I see what he's pointing at, but... I still feel like they are tracking me, like they're testing whether I understand the pattern they're making. It started around the time I began taking evening accounting courses while still working my regular bookkeeping job.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your behavior or how you're functioning in daily life over the past 10 months, aside from the specific examples you've mentioned?\n\nPatient: Yeah, there have been some pretty noticeable changes. I've even had to start asking for help to finish tasks, which is frustrating because it's work that used to be completely routine for me. It happens with normal stuff, too. If I'm just trying to explain why a ledger is off at work, my thoughts will jump from that to my tuition deadlines, and then to tax rules, and then to something completely random like studio lighting. Even if I'm not bringing up the messages or the number patterns, people still tell me they just cannot follow how I got from one point to the next.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself having trouble completing tasks or following a routine due to feeling disorganized?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. Actually, that's a big part of why I came in to get help. I've started doing things at work and school that just... don't make sense, and it stops me from getting my actual tasks done. \n\nLike, instead of doing my bookkeeping, I'll end up lining receipts all across the floor. Or I'll just sit at my desk tapping the calculator in sets of nine. Sometimes I'll even just stand by the copier with my hands held up in the air, and I'll stay like that until someone walks by and asks if I'm okay. So, yeah, it definitely makes it hard to follow a normal routine.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your thoughts are more disorganized than your speech, or is it the other way around?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure, honestly. I think... they kind of just happen together? Like, my thoughts feel scrambled, and then when I try to talk, the words come out scrambled, too. I haven't really noticed if one is worse than the other. They both just feel pretty disorganized right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your thoughts are often difficult to follow, even when you're thinking to yourself and not trying to explain them to someone else?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. I'll go from the ledger to my tuition deadlines, then to tax rules, then to studio lighting or those number patterns. It all happens in my head, too, not just when I'm talking. He'll say things like, \"check line seven\" or \"you missed the sign.\" It sounds real... like, it's clear enough that I actually look around to see who's talking to me, but there's never anyone there.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find it difficult to express your thoughts in a way that makes sense to others?\n\nPatient: Yeah, sometimes. Like... when I try to explain to my supervisor why a ledger is off, my mind just kind of goes everywhere. I'll start talking about the ledger, but then my thoughts jump to my tuition deadlines for school, and then to tax rules, and then somehow to studio lighting and the number patterns I've been seeing. I know what I'm trying to say, but people tell me they just can't follow how I got from one point to the next. It makes sense in my head, but it doesn't come out right.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your thoughts are often disjointed or hard to follow, even when you're not talking about the messages or voices you've mentioned?\n\nPatient: Yeah, exactly like I was just saying a second ago. It happens with normal stuff, too. If I'm just trying to explain why a ledger is off at work, my thoughts will jump from that to my tuition deadlines, and then to tax rules, and then to something completely random like studio lighting. Even if I'm not bringing up the messages or the number patterns, people still tell me they just cannot follow how I got from one point to the next.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself having trouble completing tasks or following a routine due to feeling disorganized?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. Actually, that's a big part of why I came in to get help. I've started doing things at work and school that just... don't make sense, and it stops me from getting my actual tasks done. \n\nLike, instead of doing my bookkeeping, I'll end up lining receipts all across the floor. Or I'll just sit at my desk tapping the calculator in sets of nine. Sometimes I'll even just stand by the copier with my hands held up in the air, and I'll stay like that until someone walks by and asks if I'm okay. So, yeah, it definitely makes it hard to follow a normal routine.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your thoughts are more disorganized than your speech, or is it the other way around?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure, honestly. I think... they kind of just happen together? Like, my thoughts feel scrambled, and then when I try to talk, the words come out scrambled, too. I haven't really noticed if one is worse than the other. They both just feel pretty disorganized right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your thoughts are often difficult to follow, even when you're thinking to yourself and not trying to explain them to someone else?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. It's like I mentioned before... even when I'm just thinking about work, like trying to figure out why a ledger is off, my brain just starts jumping around. I'll go from the ledger to my tuition deadlines, then to tax rules, then to studio lighting or those number patterns. It all happens in my head, too, not just when I'm talking. But when I do try to explain it, people tell me they just can't follow how I got from one point to the next.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voices you hear or the messages you believe you're receiving could actually be real?\n\nPatient: I mean... yeah, I do. I know it sounds strange when I say it out loud, but I get completely convinced that certain invoice totals, account numbers, and due dates at work are actual messages about me. \n\nMy supervisor has actually sat down with me and showed me the paperwork to prove they're just normal, everyday entries. And I see what he's pointing at, but... I still feel like they are tracking me, like they're testing whether I understand the pattern they're making. So, yeah, to me it feels very real.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been experiencing these thoughts and behaviors that don't make sense to you?\n\nPatient: I'd say this has been building for about 10 months now. It started around the time I began taking evening accounting courses while still working my regular bookkeeping job.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your behavior or how you're functioning in daily life over the past 10 months, aside from the specific examples you've mentioned?\n\nPatient: Yeah, there have been some pretty noticeable changes. Um, for one, I actually failed an important course module recently because I just couldn't focus on the material. And at work... well, my ledgers started coming back with some pretty serious errors. I've even had to start asking for help to finish tasks, which is frustrating because it's work that used to be completely routine for me. I just can't seem to get through it reliably anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do these strange thoughts and behaviors happen all the time, or do they come and go?\n\nPatient: Well, they don't really come and go. The voices and that feeling that the numbers are messages... that's been happening pretty consistently for about 8 months now. But even during those weeks, I still heard the voice. And I still felt completely pulled into the number patterns. So those parts never really stopped.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a significant decrease in your overall level of functioning compared to how you were doing before these strange thoughts and behaviors started?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. And sometimes, I'll just stand by the copier with my hands held up in the air... and I'll just stay like that until someone walks by and asks if I'm okay. I don't really know why I'm doing these things, so I figured I should probably talk to someone about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're choosing to do these actions, like lining up receipts or standing by the copier, or do they feel involuntary?\n\nPatient: I guess it feels more involuntary. Like I was just saying with the receipts or standing by the copier... I don't consciously decide to do it. It just sort of happens. I'll just realize I'm sitting there tapping the calculator in those sets of nine, and I didn't plan to start doing that. I just catch myself in the middle of doing these things that don't make any sense.\n\nDoctor: Do these actions cause you any distress or interfere with your daily life?\n\nPatient: Yeah, they definitely do. It's getting in the way a lot. Like, for school, I actually failed an important course module recently. And at work... my ledgers have started coming back with serious errors. I've had to start asking for help because I just can't reliably finish work that used to be completely routine for me. So, yeah, it's definitely causing some problems.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any recurring thoughts that you can't seem to get out of your head?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. Um, I know it sounds really strange, but... I get convinced that certain invoice totals, account numbers, and due dates at work are actually messages about me. It just gets stuck in my head. And even when my supervisor sits down with me and shows me that they're just normal, everyday entries, I... I still feel like they're tracking whether I understand the pattern. I just can't seem to shake that thought.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever hear voices or sounds that other people can't hear?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I actually do. Um, sometimes when I'm just sitting alone at my desk, or when I'm walking home from class, I'll hear a man's voice. He'll say things like, \"check line seven\" or \"you missed the sign.\" It sounds real... like, it's clear enough that I actually look around to see who's talking to me, but there's never anyone there.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find it difficult to express your thoughts in a way that makes sense to others?\n\nPatient: Yeah, sometimes. Like... when I try to explain to my supervisor why a ledger is off, my mind just kind of goes everywhere. I'll start talking about the ledger, but then my thoughts jump to my tuition deadlines for school, and then to tax rules, and then somehow to studio lighting and the number patterns I've been seeing. I know what I'm trying to say, but people tell me they just can't follow how I got from one point to the next. It makes sense in my head, but it doesn't come out right.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your thoughts are often disjointed or hard to follow, even when you're not talking about the messages or voices you've mentioned?\n\nPatient: Yeah, exactly like I was just saying a second ago. It happens with normal stuff, too. If I'm just trying to explain why a ledger is off at work, my thoughts will jump from that to my tuition deadlines, and then to tax rules, and then to something completely random like studio lighting. Even if I'm not bringing up the messages or the number patterns, people still tell me they just cannot follow how I got from one point to the next.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself having trouble completing tasks or following a routine due to feeling disorganized?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. Actually, that's a big part of why I came in to get help. I've started doing things at work and school that just... don't make sense, and it stops me from getting my actual tasks done. \n\nLike, instead of doing my bookkeeping, I'll end up lining receipts all across the floor. Or I'll just sit at my desk tapping the calculator in sets of nine. Sometimes I'll even just stand by the copier with my hands held up in the air, and I'll stay like that until someone walks by and asks if I'm okay. So, yeah, it definitely makes it hard to follow a normal routine.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your thoughts are more disorganized than your speech, or is it the other way around?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure, honestly. I think... they kind of just happen together? Like, my thoughts feel scrambled, and then when I try to talk, the words come out scrambled, too. I haven't really noticed if one is worse than the other. They both just feel pretty disorganized right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your thoughts are often difficult to follow, even when you're thinking to yourself and not trying to explain them to someone else?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. It's like I mentioned before... even when I'm just thinking about work, like trying to figure out why a ledger is off, my brain just starts jumping around. I'll go from the ledger to my tuition deadlines, then to tax rules, then to studio lighting or those number patterns. It all happens in my head, too, not just when I'm talking. But when I do try to explain it, people tell me they just can't follow how I got from one point to the next.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voices you hear or the messages you believe you're receiving could actually be real?\n\nPatient: I mean... yeah, I do. I know it sounds strange when I say it out loud, but I get completely convinced that certain invoice totals, account numbers, and due dates at work are actual messages about me. \n\nMy supervisor has actually sat down with me and showed me the paperwork to prove they're just normal, everyday entries. And I see what he's pointing at, but... I still feel like they are tracking me, like they're testing whether I understand the pattern they're making. So, yeah, to me it feels very real.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been experiencing these thoughts and behaviors that don't make sense to you?\n\nPatient: I'd say this has been building for about 10 months now. It started around the time I began taking evening accounting courses while still working my regular bookkeeping job.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your behavior or how you're functioning in daily life over the past 10 months, aside from the specific examples you've mentioned?\n\nPatient: Yeah, there have been some pretty noticeable changes. Um, for one, I actually failed an important course module recently because I just couldn't focus on the material. And at work... well, my ledgers started coming back with some pretty serious errors. I've even had to start asking for help to finish tasks, which is frustrating because it's work that used to be completely routine for me. I just can't seem to get through it reliably anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do these strange thoughts and behaviors happen all the time, or do they come and go?\n\nPatient: Well, they don't really come and go. The voices and that feeling that the numbers are messages... that's been happening pretty consistently for about 8 months now. It's just kind of always there.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time where these strange thoughts and behaviors completely stopped?\n\nPatient: No, not completely. I mean... there was a stretch of a few weeks where things settled down a bit in other ways. Like, I was sleeping more normally again, and I didn't feel so, um, wired or especially energized. But even during those weeks, I still heard the voice. And I still felt completely pulled into the number patterns. So those parts never really stopped.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a significant decrease in your overall level of functioning compared to how you were doing before these strange thoughts and behaviors started?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. I mean, like I mentioned a few minutes ago, that's exactly what's happening. Before all this started, I was doing fine. But now... well, failing that course module and having those serious errors on my ledgers, it's a big drop for me. Having to ask for help just to get through routine work that I used to do in my sleep... yeah, it's definitely a significant decrease in how I'm functioning compared to before.\n\nDoctor: Have your strange thoughts and behaviors been continuous or have you had any periods where they were significantly less intense or absent for at least a few days?\n\nPatient: Um, I think I kind of just answered that, actually. Like I was saying a minute ago... no, they haven't stopped or really faded at all. Even during those couple of weeks where my sleep got better and I wasn't so wired, the voice and the numbers were still there just as strong. So, no, there hasn't been any break where it was absent, even for a few days. It's just been continuous.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.
